## TOP_CROSSWALK_PROPERTIES

| ID | Property | Mature form | Trigger | Cheap path |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P001 | Ground stakeholder and mission intent in authoritative, current evidence | A living intent model whose authority, recency, conflicts, measures and affected decisions are explicit; revalidate after material change. | Multiple stakeholders; costly or irreversible design; regulated/contractual commitments; uncertain mission; long lifecycle; changed operating context. | For a small reversible task with one empowered user and immediate feedback, a dated goal statement plus direct confirmation is enough. |
| P002 | Separate validated need and problem from proposed solution | Maintain an explicit need–solution boundary, with exceptions justified by named authority and revisited when constraints change. | Early concept selection; competing technologies; high switching cost; ambiguous requests; procurement language that embeds a product. | Where the mandated implementation is genuinely fixed by law, interoperability or owner authority, record that constraint and skip artificial alternative generation. |
| P006 | Preserve requirement provenance, authority and rationale | Semantic provenance that supports legitimacy and change decisions, not merely backward hyperlinks. | Large or long-lived baselines; regulated/contracted work; reused requirements; multiple issuers; frequent change. | For a directly confirmed, low-cost request, source, date and owner may be sufficient. |
| P007 | Refine requirements progressively under controlled baselines | Progressive commitment: requirements are neither presumed complete nor left unowned; current authority and impact are always visible. | Uncertain or novel systems; changing user needs; software-intensive development; long programmes; learning via prototypes/integration. | Stable, well-understood repeat work may baseline early and manage only exceptions. |
| P010 | Manage assumptions as owned, testable and expiring engineering claims | Assumption currentness is a first-class relation separate from ordinary traceability, with explicit falsification and change propagation. | Reuse; supplier boundaries; uncertain environment; models/simulation; long lifecycle; autonomous constituents. | For local reversible work, state the one or two assumptions that could overturn the decision and test them directly. |
| P011 | Maintain semantic, consumer-driven traceability | A current semantic relation with a named consumer and invalidation rules, independent of spreadsheet or model tooling. | Many artefacts/teams; high change cost; certification; maintenance; cross-level impact analysis. | For small co-located work, deterministic naming, version control and direct inspection may answer the needed question more cheaply. |
| P014 | Record architecture decisions, alternatives and rationale | Decision-centred architecture knowledge linked to current implementation and evidence; diagrams are optional views. | Long-lived systems; personnel turnover; costly choices; multiple alternatives; reuse/platform decisions. | For a small reversible choice, a concise decision note with the rejected alternative and trigger is sufficient. |
| P019 | Generate real alternatives before consequential commitment | Alternative generation is a falsifiable search for materially different feasible means, scaled to decision consequence. | Architecture down-select; technology selection; high irreversibility; uncertain solution space. | When only one option is legally/physically possible, verify that constraint rather than fabricate alternatives. |
| P020 | Test trade decisions for uncertainty, sensitivity and false precision | Sensitivity-first decision analysis with explicit judgement, uncertainty and robustness; matrices are optional calculators. | Multi-attribute choices; uncertain data; close alternatives; political stakeholder weights. | A cheap deterministic discriminator can end the study immediately when one option violates a hard constraint or clearly dominates. |
| P023 | Specify interface semantics, timing, state and failure contracts | A current behavioural/semantic contract whose precision is proportionate to risk; documents, schemas, types and tests are interchangeable carriers. | Cross-team integration; data exchange; real-time systems; safety/performance coupling; supplier APIs. | For obvious low-risk local interfaces, executable types/tests and one shared example may be enough. |
| P024 | Verify interface assumptions and currentness | Interface assurance couples current semantic agreement, configuration identity and composition evidence. | Frequent interface evolution; independent suppliers; reused components; high-consequence interactions. | Stable local interfaces can rely on automated compatibility tests plus change notification. |
| P026 | Establish authoritative configuration identity and baselines | Identity sufficient to answer exactly what was decided, built, integrated, tested, delivered and operated—without mandating frozen documents. | Multiple teams/suppliers; releases/builds; physical units; certification; long lifecycle; evidence reuse. | For a small reversible prototype, an immutable build identifier, dependency lock and dated test record may suffice. |
| P028 | Propagate change impact across technical relationships | Evidence-bearing impact analysis that admits uncertainty and feeds implementation, integration and revalidation—not a form approval. | High coupling; shared resources; reused assets; safety/performance effects; cross-organisation changes. | For a local isolated change, deterministic dependency analysis plus focused regression is the cheap path. |
| P029 | Re-establish evidence currentness after change | Evidence is a versioned claim with an applicability boundary and refresh rule, not a timeless attachment. | Any claim carried across versions; certification; reused analyses/models; operational updates. | No-op or provably irrelevant changes can retain evidence with a concise impact proof. |
| P030 | Maintain parity among model, implementation, test, data and environment | Governed representation parity with explicit, risk-bounded inconsistencies—never an unsupported claim that one repository equals reality. | MBSE/digital thread; generated artefacts; AI/ML datasets; simulation; multi-tool programmes. | For a compact code-centric system, source, dependency lock, tests and environment manifest may form the whole parity set. |
| P032 | Integrate early, incrementally and at risky boundaries | Integration cadence proportional to risk and reversibility, with escalating fidelity and truthful limits. | Novel interactions; distributed teams; change-prone software; high coupling; long supplier chains. | Where physical integration is destructive or extraordinarily costly, use earlier models/fixtures and fewer evidence-rich physical events. |
| P035 | Reason about emergent and whole-system interactions | Whole-system interaction hypotheses tied to testable evidence and boundaries, not vague complexity language. | Complex adaptive systems; autonomy; dense coupling; SoS; humans in operation; uncertain environment. | Do not invoke 'emergence' for every dependency: deterministic interface and resource checks should be exhausted first. |
| P036 | Distinguish verification from validation | Two explicit assurance questions with distinct evidence and consumers, applied iteratively across levels. | Any consequential system; especially where requirements may be wrong, incomplete or stale. | For tiny direct-user work, one feedback event may simultaneously inform both questions, but the two claims remain distinct. |
| P038 | Select evidence methods fit to the claim and decision | Claim-centred evidence design with explicit adequacy, limitations and residual uncertainty; no method hierarchy is universal. | Large requirement sets; hazardous/expensive testing; analytical certification; model-based evidence. | Use the cheapest deterministic discriminator that genuinely settles the claim. |
| P039 | Validate with real users, operations and mission context | Continuous, context-aware validation connected to authoritative need and post-deployment evidence. | Human-facing systems; new workflows; mission capability; autonomous/AI systems; complex operations. | For a small direct-user service, observe actual use and incorporate rapid feedback rather than stage a formal validation event. |
| P040 | Preserve evidence identity, coverage, limitations and anomalies | Evidence as a reproducible, bounded claim rather than a status cell or signed attachment. | Certification, long lifecycle, reused tests, many variants, distributed test organisations. | For low-risk work, a reproducible command, immutable result and concise limitation note may be enough. |
| P041 | Establish model credibility for a specified intended use | Risk- and use-bounded model credibility with explicit prohibition against unsupported reuse. | Models replace or shape costly tests; autonomous systems; digital twins; safety/performance decisions. | For low-consequence exploratory models, label them as exploratory and avoid formal credibility programmes. |
| P044 | Tailor systems-engineering depth to consequence, complexity, uncertainty and reversibility | A trigger-based assurance architecture with explicit escalation and de-escalation, not project-size folklore or blanket waivers. | Every project; especially heterogeneous portfolios and mixed hardware/software. | Local reversible work should use direct evidence, short records and immediate feedback unless a trigger appears. |
| P050 | Reuse evidence only within explicit applicability boundaries | Evidence reuse as a bounded inference with explicit source identity, delta analysis and authority—not document inheritance. | Product families; software updates; supplier reuse; models; digital threads; certification-heavy domains. | Rerun a cheap decisive test rather than writing an elaborate similarity argument. |
| P059 | Feed integration and operational evidence back into intent, requirements and architecture | A closed learning loop from composition/use to governing intent and decisions, with transparent rebaselining. | Novel systems; progressive requirements; operational pilots; emergent interactions; changed use. | A straightforward implementation defect with unchanged premise can be fixed locally. |
| P060 | Maintain accessible, current technical data and decision records | Technical knowledge continuity as governed access and identity, not accumulation or one-tool centralisation. | Long lifecycle; supplier turnover; regulated evidence; multi-tool environment; sustainment. | For small work, a well-structured repository with immutable history and clear current pointers may be enough. |

## COMPLETE_CROSSWALK_WORTHY_PROPERTY_RECORDS

### P001 — Ground stakeholder and mission intent in authoritative, current evidence

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Solution success is asserted against an unauthorised, stale or internally invented notion of value.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A living intent model whose authority, recency, conflicts, measures and affected decisions are explicit; revalidate after material change.
- **TRIGGER:** Multiple stakeholders; costly or irreversible design; regulated/contractual commitments; uncertain mission; long lifecycle; changed operating context.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible task with one empowered user and immediate feedback, a dated goal statement plus direct confirmation is enough.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** A named person, body, law, contract or mission authority must be able to legitimise or change the governing intent; facilitation is not authority.
- **CRITICISMS:** Stakeholder workshops and CONOPS documents can create consensus theatre; stated requests may reflect politics, solution bias or absent users.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** CONOPS, mission statements, stakeholder registers and workshops are optional carriers. The property is current, authoritative intent that governs and can be challenged.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S024`, `S064`, `S085`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S024`, `S003`, `S085`, `S064`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Access to representative stakeholders or mission evidence; explicit system boundary and intended lifecycle; a means to resolve or preserve disagreement.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** The governing need, stakeholder classes, intended use and current context must be identifiable; latent/absent stakeholders are a declared limitation.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The intent record, source and applicable system/configuration/time horizon must be versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** External actors, environments and ecosystem dependencies that affect intent must be within scope.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Validation evidence must be capable of challenging the stated need and intended-use model.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** STRONG_WITH_TRIGGER_AND_CHEAP_PATH
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Stable baselines versus changing intent** — balancing properties: P026, P027; hybrid: Time-bounded/incremental baselines with named authority, impact propagation and revalidation.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Ground stakeholder and mission intent in authoritative, current evidence” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can solution success is asserted against an unauthorised, stale or internally invented notion of value remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Does the target distinguish a stakeholder request from a validated need and from a proposed architecture?
- Can it show why the mission/intended-use statement remains authoritative and current?

#### STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Ground stakeholder and mission intent in authoritative, current evidence
- **MISSION_OR_NEED:** The governing need, stakeholder classes, intended use and current context must be identifiable; latent/absent stakeholders are a declared limitation.
- **STAKEHOLDERS_AND_LIFECYCLE_STANDING:** Identify requesters, users, operators, maintainers, acquirers, regulators, support/disposal actors and affected external parties only where they change the governing claim or consequence.
- **AUTHORITY:** A named person, body, law, contract or mission authority must be able to legitimise or change the governing intent; facilitation is not authority.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_CONTEXT:** State the intended use, operational conditions, external actors and validity interval relevant to this property; no generic stakeholder list substitutes.
- **CONSTRAINTS_AND_ASSUMPTIONS:** Distinguish authorised constraints from preferences and proposed solutions; own and expire material assumptions.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Re-establish after mission, authority, use-context, interface or configuration change.
- **VALIDATION:** Confirm that the governing stakeholder/mission claim remains legitimate and current enough for the decision.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible task with one empowered user and immediate feedback, a dated goal statement plus direct confirmation is enough.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A living intent model whose authority, recency, conflicts, measures and affected decisions are explicit; revalidate after material change.

#### REQUIREMENTS_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Ground stakeholder and mission intent in authoritative, current evidence
- **NEED_OR_SOURCE:** Must resolve to current stakeholder/mission evidence or an explicitly authorised constraint; for rejected candidates, this is the missing stronger relation.
- **REQUIREMENT_FORM:** Structured text, model, contract, scenario, constraint, testable property or other fit-for-purpose representation; no mandatory template.
- **PROVENANCE:** Source identity, version, rationale and derivation are required where the property can govern downstream work.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Material assumptions are explicit, owned and linked to falsification/expiry conditions.
- **AUTHORITY:** A named person, body, law, contract or mission authority must be able to legitimise or change the governing intent; facilitation is not authority.
- **TRACEABILITY:** Only semantic relations needed by source, impact, implementation or evidence consumers; link presence alone is insufficient.
- **VALIDATION_PRECONDITION:** The governing need and intended use remain current enough to justify the requirement.
- **VERIFICATION_PRECONDITION:** The claim and applicable configuration are precise enough for a discriminating evidence method.
- **CHANGE_PROFILE:** Versioned; impacts and evidence are invalidated/reconfirmed after relevant source, assumption, architecture, interface or configuration change.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible task with one empowered user and immediate feedback, a dated goal statement plus direct confirmation is enough.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A living intent model whose authority, recency, conflicts, measures and affected decisions are explicit; revalidate after material change.

#### VERIFICATION_VALIDATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Ground stakeholder and mission intent in authoritative, current evidence
- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **CLAIM:** Component teams can optimise a technically impressive solution that does not solve the governing mission or current stakeholder problem.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the applicable specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does the result remain fit for the current intended use when this property affects that judgement?
- **METHOD:** Validation evidence must be capable of challenging the stated need and intended-use model.
- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** The intent record, source and applicable system/configuration/time horizon must be versioned.
- **INDEPENDENCE:** Proportionate to consequence, producer incentive and claim opacity.
- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** No representation can prove latent future needs are complete; authority may be contested.
- **ANOMALIES:** Solution-biased framing; token stakeholder participation; stale authority; proxy outcomes; hidden conflicts; retrospective rationalisation.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Reconfirm after relevant source, configuration, interface, environment or use change.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible task with one empowered user and immediate feedback, a dated goal statement plus direct confirmation is enough.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A living intent model whose authority, recency, conflicts, measures and affected decisions are explicit; revalidate after material change.

#### MODEL_OR_DIGITAL_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Ground stakeholder and mission intent in authoritative, current evidence
- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Mission owner, product owner, architecture authority, requirements authority and validation authority.
- **AUTHORITATIVE_SCOPE:** A named person, body, law, contract or mission authority must be able to legitimise or change the governing intent; facilitation is not authority.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** No representation can prove latent future needs are complete; authority may be contested.
- **PROVENANCE:** Hall's problem-definition and goal-setting tradition; NASA stakeholder-expectations practice; requirements-engineering work on environment and agreement.
- **PARITY:** The intent record, source and applicable system/configuration/time horizon must be versioned.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** External actors, environments and ecosystem dependencies that affect intent must be within scope.
- **VALIDATION:** Validation evidence must be capable of challenging the stated need and intended-use model.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Signal when source, model, data, configuration or use becomes stale.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible task with one empowered user and immediate feedback, a dated goal statement plus direct confirmation is enough.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A living intent model whose authority, recency, conflicts, measures and affected decisions are explicit; revalidate after material change.

</details>

### P002 — Separate validated need and problem from proposed solution

- **FAILURE_MODE:** A design is verified perfectly against a requirement that merely restates the preselected solution.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Maintain an explicit need–solution boundary, with exceptions justified by named authority and revisited when constraints change.
- **TRIGGER:** Early concept selection; competing technologies; high switching cost; ambiguous requests; procurement language that embeds a product.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Where the mandated implementation is genuinely fixed by law, interoperability or owner authority, record that constraint and skip artificial alternative generation.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** A named person, body, law, contract or mission authority must be able to legitimise or change the governing intent; facilitation is not authority.
- **CRITICISMS:** The distinction is not absolute: some needs are only discoverable through prototypes, and architecture can reshape feasible goals.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** CONOPS, mission statements, stakeholder registers and workshops are optional carriers. The property is current, authoritative intent that governs and can be challenged.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S024`, `S039`, `S063`, `S064`, `S073`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S024`, `S039`, `S063`, `S073`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Access to representative stakeholders or mission evidence; explicit system boundary and intended lifecycle; a means to resolve or preserve disagreement.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** The governing need, stakeholder classes, intended use and current context must be identifiable; latent/absent stakeholders are a declared limitation.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The intent record, source and applicable system/configuration/time horizon must be versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** External actors, environments and ecosystem dependencies that affect intent must be within scope.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Validation evidence must be capable of challenging the stated need and intended-use model.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Design freedom versus requirement constraint** — balancing properties: P006, P012; hybrid: Specify need/performance/constraints before solution; defer allocations; preserve alternatives until evidence discriminates.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Separate validated need and problem from proposed solution” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can a design is verified perfectly against a requirement that merely restates the preselected solution remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Does the target distinguish a stakeholder request from a validated need and from a proposed architecture?
- Can it show why the mission/intended-use statement remains authoritative and current?

### P003 — Map stakeholder authority, conflicts and lifecycle standing

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Conflicts are hidden inside ambiguous requirements and reappear as late acceptance or operational failure.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A challengeable authority map, not a contact list; unresolved conflicts remain visible and constrain baselining.
- **TRIGGER:** Distributed ownership; multiple customer classes; safety/regulatory duties; operator/maintainer burden; systems of systems.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Single-user reversible work can use direct owner confirmation without a formal register.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** A named person, body, law, contract or mission authority must be able to legitimise or change the governing intent; facilitation is not authority.
- **CRITICISMS:** Stakeholder mapping can legitimise whoever was easiest to interview and can manufacture false consensus.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** CONOPS, mission statements, stakeholder registers and workshops are optional carriers. The property is current, authoritative intent that governs and can be challenged.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S060`, `S061`, `S065`, `S111`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S060`, `S061`, `S003`, `S111`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Access to representative stakeholders or mission evidence; explicit system boundary and intended lifecycle; a means to resolve or preserve disagreement.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** The governing need, stakeholder classes, intended use and current context must be identifiable; latent/absent stakeholders are a declared limitation.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The intent record, source and applicable system/configuration/time horizon must be versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** External actors, environments and ecosystem dependencies that affect intent must be within scope.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Validation evidence must be capable of challenging the stated need and intended-use model.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG

- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

### P004 — Model intended use, operational states and environment

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Acceptance testing exercises a laboratory abstraction rather than the operational problem.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A maintained operational/use-context model tied to requirements, architecture, interfaces and validation evidence; format is optional.
- **TRIGGER:** Context-sensitive behaviour; many modes; human/organisational interaction; remote or hazardous operation; degraded states.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For simple products, a handful of concrete usage examples and explicit environment bounds may replace a formal CONOPS.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** A named person, body, law, contract or mission authority must be able to legitimise or change the governing intent; facilitation is not authority.
- **CRITICISMS:** Operational documents become speculative narratives when not checked with real users or evidence; exhaustive scenario catalogues are impossible.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** CONOPS, mission statements, stakeholder registers and workshops are optional carriers. The property is current, authoritative intent that governs and can be challenged.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S085`, `S111`, `S114`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S085`, `S111`, `S114`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Access to representative stakeholders or mission evidence; explicit system boundary and intended lifecycle; a means to resolve or preserve disagreement.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** The governing need, stakeholder classes, intended use and current context must be identifiable; latent/absent stakeholders are a declared limitation.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The intent record, source and applicable system/configuration/time horizon must be versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** External actors, environments and ecosystem dependencies that affect intent must be within scope.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Validation evidence must be capable of challenging the stated need and intended-use model.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** STRONG_WITH_TRIGGER_AND_CHEAP_PATH
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- No specific ledger tension mapped; generic cost/benefit and tailoring tension still applies.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Model intended use, operational states and environment” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can acceptance testing exercises a laboratory abstraction rather than the operational problem remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Does the target distinguish a stakeholder request from a validated need and from a proposed architecture?
- Can it show why the mission/intended-use statement remains authoritative and current?

### P005 — Govern outcome, mission and effectiveness measures

- **FAILURE_MODE:** A dashboard stays green while the delivered capability remains unusable or strategically irrelevant.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A small set of validated, decision-bearing measures with provenance, uncertainty and anti-gaming checks; retire measures without consumers.
- **TRIGGER:** Architecture trades; mission/capability portfolios; performance competition; changing operational priorities.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Where direct user feedback immediately settles value, use it instead of constructing a metric hierarchy.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** A named person, body, law, contract or mission authority must be able to legitimise or change the governing intent; facilitation is not authority.
- **CRITICISMS:** Goodhart effects, proxy drift, arbitrary aggregation and inaccessible measurement can turn MOEs into theatre.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** CONOPS, mission statements, stakeholder registers and workshops are optional carriers. The property is current, authoritative intent that governs and can be challenged.

- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** External actors, environments and ecosystem dependencies that affect intent must be within scope.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Validation evidence must be capable of challenging the stated need and intended-use model.

- **PARITY:** The intent record, source and applicable system/configuration/time horizon must be versioned.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** External actors, environments and ecosystem dependencies that affect intent must be within scope.
- **VALIDATION:** Validation evidence must be capable of challenging the stated need and intended-use model.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Signal when source, model, data, configuration or use becomes stale.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Where direct user feedback immediately settles value, use it instead of constructing a metric hierarchy.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A small set of validated, decision-bearing measures with provenance, uncertainty and anti-gaming checks; retire measures without consumers.

</details>

### P006 — Preserve requirement provenance, authority and rationale

- **FAILURE_MODE:** An arbitrary or obsolete requirement consumes design, test and certification effort.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Semantic provenance that supports legitimacy and change decisions, not merely backward hyperlinks.
- **TRIGGER:** Large or long-lived baselines; regulated/contracted work; reused requirements; multiple issuers; frequent change.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a directly confirmed, low-cost request, source, date and owner may be sufficient.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **CRITICISMS:** Maintaining rationale is costly and can expose politically sensitive disagreement; source authority can still be illegitimate.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Templates, shall-statements, databases and RTMs are optional. The property is a justified, current and usable constraint/claim.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S010`, `S062`, `S067`, `S003`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S067`, `S010`, `S062`, `S003`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** A governed need/source model, identifiable authors/owners, change mechanism and downstream design/V&V consumers.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Requirements must inherit legitimacy from validated need, contract/regulation or explicitly authorised design constraints.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Each requirement set and relation must identify version, baseline, applicability and supersession.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Allocations and cross-boundary requirements require explicit interface ownership and composition checks.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Feasibility and verification method must be considered, while validation remains separate from testability.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Requirements completeness versus discovery** — balancing properties: P007, P010, P059; hybrid: Progressive elaboration; assumptions; scenarios; unknown/TBD inventory; risk-ordered refinement.
- **Design freedom versus requirement constraint** — balancing properties: P002, P019, P021; hybrid: Specify need/performance/constraints before solution; defer allocations; preserve alternatives until evidence discriminates.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Preserve requirement provenance, authority and rationale” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can an arbitrary or obsolete requirement consumes design, test and certification effort remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Can a documented and traceable requirement still be unjustified, semantically wrong or stale?
- Does progressive refinement propagate through allocations, decisions and V&V before closure?

- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does it fulfil the current intended use/need in the relevant context?

- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** Representative enough for the claim; provenance, fidelity and limitations explicit.

- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** Permissible decisions, assumptions, uncertainty, fidelity and caveats.
- **PROVENANCE:** Model/metamodel/tool/version, data and transformations are identified.
- **PARITY:** Implementation/physical/test/environment relations and permitted divergence are explicit.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Semantic mapping and transformation losses are tested; open export/API and exit path considered.
- **VALIDATION:** Credibility evidence proportionate to decision consequence.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Refresh latency, event triggers and stale-state signalling are explicit.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a directly confirmed, low-cost request, source, date and owner may be sufficient.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Semantic provenance that supports legitimacy and change decisions, not merely backward hyperlinks.

### P007 — Refine requirements progressively under controlled baselines

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Teams either lock in the wrong system or continuously churn without a trustworthy current definition.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Progressive commitment: requirements are neither presumed complete nor left unowned; current authority and impact are always visible.
- **TRIGGER:** Uncertain or novel systems; changing user needs; software-intensive development; long programmes; learning via prototypes/integration.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Stable, well-understood repeat work may baseline early and manage only exceptions.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **CRITICISMS:** Iteration can be invoked to excuse weak analysis; baselines can become expensive freezes or meaningless snapshots.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Templates, shall-statements, databases and RTMs are optional. The property is a justified, current and usable constraint/claim.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S001`, `S009`, `S061`, `S063`, `S108`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S001`, `S009`, `S061`, `S063`

- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Allocations and cross-boundary requirements require explicit interface ownership and composition checks.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Feasibility and verification method must be considered, while validation remains separate from testability.

- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Upfront architecture versus evolutionary learning** — balancing properties: P013, P014, P017; hybrid: Set-based alternatives; minimum architecture runway; explicit decision-reopening triggers; incremental integration.

### P008 — Apply requirement quality criteria as decision aids, not truth certificates

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Grammar/format compliance masks semantic error or removes useful contextual nuance.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-calibrated checks with false-positive/negative awareness and semantic adjudication.
- **TRIGGER:** Large natural-language baselines; distributed authors; regulated verification; repeated defect classes.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Small sets can use peer challenge and concrete examples instead of automated rule suites.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **CRITICISMS:** Literature proposes many overlapping attributes with weak definitions; automated smells show variable precision and recall.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Templates, shall-statements, databases and RTMs are optional. The property is a justified, current and usable constraint/claim.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S010`, `S059`, `S066`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S059`, `S010`, `S066`

- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** A governed need/source model, identifiable authors/owners, change mechanism and downstream design/V&V consumers.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Requirements must inherit legitimacy from validated need, contract/regulation or explicitly authorised design constraints.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Each requirement set and relation must identify version, baseline, applicability and supersession.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Allocations and cross-boundary requirements require explicit interface ownership and composition checks.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Feasibility and verification method must be considered, while validation remains separate from testability.

- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Requirements completeness versus discovery** — balancing properties: P007, P010, P059; hybrid: Progressive elaboration; assumptions; scenarios; unknown/TBD inventory; risk-ordered refinement.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Apply requirement quality criteria as decision aids, not truth certificates” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can grammar/format compliance masks semantic error or removes useful contextual nuance remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Can a documented and traceable requirement still be unjustified, semantically wrong or stale?
- Does progressive refinement propagate through allocations, decisions and V&V before closure?

#### STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Apply requirement quality criteria as decision aids, not truth certificates
- **MISSION_OR_NEED:** Requirements must inherit legitimacy from validated need, contract/regulation or explicitly authorised design constraints.
- **STAKEHOLDERS_AND_LIFECYCLE_STANDING:** Identify requesters, users, operators, maintainers, acquirers, regulators, support/disposal actors and affected external parties only where they change the governing claim or consequence.
- **AUTHORITY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_CONTEXT:** State the intended use, operational conditions, external actors and validity interval relevant to this property; no generic stakeholder list substitutes.
- **CONSTRAINTS_AND_ASSUMPTIONS:** Distinguish authorised constraints from preferences and proposed solutions; own and expire material assumptions.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Re-establish after mission, authority, use-context, interface or configuration change.
- **VALIDATION:** Confirm that the governing stakeholder/mission claim remains legitimate and current enough for the decision.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Small sets can use peer challenge and concrete examples instead of automated rule suites.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-calibrated checks with false-positive/negative awareness and semantic adjudication.

#### REQUIREMENTS_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Apply requirement quality criteria as decision aids, not truth certificates
- **NEED_OR_SOURCE:** Must resolve to current stakeholder/mission evidence or an explicitly authorised constraint; for rejected candidates, this is the missing stronger relation.
- **REQUIREMENT_FORM:** Structured text, model, contract, scenario, constraint, testable property or other fit-for-purpose representation; no mandatory template.
- **PROVENANCE:** Source identity, version, rationale and derivation are required where the property can govern downstream work.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Material assumptions are explicit, owned and linked to falsification/expiry conditions.
- **AUTHORITY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **TRACEABILITY:** Only semantic relations needed by source, impact, implementation or evidence consumers; link presence alone is insufficient.
- **VALIDATION_PRECONDITION:** The governing need and intended use remain current enough to justify the requirement.
- **VERIFICATION_PRECONDITION:** The claim and applicable configuration are precise enough for a discriminating evidence method.
- **CHANGE_PROFILE:** Versioned; impacts and evidence are invalidated/reconfirmed after relevant source, assumption, architecture, interface or configuration change.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Small sets can use peer challenge and concrete examples instead of automated rule suites.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-calibrated checks with false-positive/negative awareness and semantic adjudication.

#### ARCHITECTURE_DECISION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Apply requirement quality criteria as decision aids, not truth certificates
- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **DRIVERS:** Large natural-language baselines; distributed authors; regulated verification; repeated defect classes.
- **DECISION:** Requirements authority, architect, implementing team, interface owner, change authority, verifier and validator.
- **ALTERNATIVES:** Do not invent an architecture decision unless the property changes a consequential option.
- **RATIONALE:** Use quality checks to flag candidate defects; resolve them against source, domain, architecture, interface and V&V evidence; distinguish local statement quality from set/system adequacy.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** A governed need/source model, identifiable authors/owners, change mechanism and downstream design/V&V consumers.
- **AUTHORITY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **CONFIGURATION:** Each requirement set and relation must identify version, baseline, applicability and supersession.
- **EVALUATION:** Feasibility and verification method must be considered, while validation remains separate from testability.
- **REOPEN_TRIGGER:** Reopen when the source, assumption, interface, configuration or evidence boundary changes materially.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Small sets can use peer challenge and concrete examples instead of automated rule suites.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-calibrated checks with false-positive/negative awareness and semantic adjudication.

- **IDENTIFIED_ITEMS:** All representations/products/environments whose identity can change the claim or decision.

- **IMPACT_SCOPE:** Requirements, architecture, assumptions, interfaces, suppliers, models, code/hardware, tests, operations and claims as material.

- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **INTEGRATION_OBJECTIVE:** Reduces the probability, detection latency or consequence of: Grammar/format compliance masks semantic error or removes useful contextual nuance. It should also improve the quality or speed of the named decision consumer; otherwise it should be retired or simplified.
- **SEQUENCE:** Order work by risk, dependency and learning only where composition is part of the failure mechanism.
- **ENTRY_IDENTITY:** Each requirement set and relation must identify version, baseline, applicability and supersession.
- **ENVIRONMENT_OR_SURROGATE:** Feasibility and verification method must be considered, while validation remains separate from testability.

#### VERIFICATION_VALIDATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Apply requirement quality criteria as decision aids, not truth certificates
- **CLAIM:** A specific requirement, architecture/interface proposition, model-use claim, mission/use claim or change-equivalence claim.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does it fulfil the current intended use/need in the relevant context?
- **METHOD:** Test, analysis, inspection, demonstration, simulation, operational evidence or combination selected for discrimination and cost.
- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** Representative enough for the claim; provenance, fidelity and limitations explicit.
- **INDEPENDENCE:** Proportionate to consequence, producer incentives and claim uncertainty.
- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** Coverage is stated but never equated with absence of unknown behaviour.
- **ANOMALIES:** Contradictory and unresolved evidence remains visible.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Bound to configuration, assumptions, intended use and change impact.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Small sets can use peer challenge and concrete examples instead of automated rule suites.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-calibrated checks with false-positive/negative awareness and semantic adjudication.

#### MODEL_OR_DIGITAL_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Apply requirement quality criteria as decision aids, not truth certificates
- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Named question, decision or evidence use; no value claim from existence or volume.
- **AUTHORITATIVE_SCOPE:** Authority is claim- and use-specific, never automatic truth.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** Permissible decisions, assumptions, uncertainty, fidelity and caveats.
- **PROVENANCE:** Model/metamodel/tool/version, data and transformations are identified.
- **PARITY:** Implementation/physical/test/environment relations and permitted divergence are explicit.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Semantic mapping and transformation losses are tested; open export/API and exit path considered.
- **VALIDATION:** Credibility evidence proportionate to decision consequence.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Refresh latency, event triggers and stale-state signalling are explicit.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Small sets can use peer challenge and concrete examples instead of automated rule suites.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-calibrated checks with false-positive/negative awareness and semantic adjudication.

### P009 — Expose requirement priority, conflict and volatility

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Teams optimise local compliance while sacrificing higher-value or more authoritative outcomes.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Priority and conflict are explicit engineering data with authority and uncertainty, not undocumented meeting outcomes.
- **TRIGGER:** Constrained resources; competing stakeholders; evolving markets/missions; architecture trade-offs.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** When one authoritative requirement set is small and stable, a simple ordered list and exception log suffice.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **CRITICISMS:** Numerical priorities can create false precision or be gamed by stakeholders; volatility labels can stigmatise legitimate learning.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Templates, shall-statements, databases and RTMs are optional. The property is a justified, current and usable constraint/claim.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S061`, `S063`, `S065`, `S108`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S061`, `S063`, `S108`, `S065`

- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Feasibility and verification method must be considered, while validation remains separate from testability.

### P010 — Manage assumptions as owned, testable and expiring engineering claims

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Ariane-style reuse, interface mismatch or invalid model inference occurs while all explicit requirements appear satisfied.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Assumption currentness is a first-class relation separate from ordinary traceability, with explicit falsification and change propagation.
- **TRIGGER:** Reuse; supplier boundaries; uncertain environment; models/simulation; long lifecycle; autonomous constituents.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For local reversible work, state the one or two assumptions that could overturn the decision and test them directly.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Someone must be authorised to create, challenge, expire and waive the relation or assumption.
- **CRITICISMS:** Assumption registers become dumping grounds; unknown unknowns cannot be listed; owners may lack authority to observe the condition.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Trace matrices and assumption logs are optional. The property is a current, meaningful and challengeable relation.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S064`, `S067`, `S077`, `S017`, `S044`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S044`, `S067`, `S017`, `S064`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined relation types, owners, source identities, current configurations and real decisions that consume the relation.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Relations must ultimately connect to authorised needs or explicitly bounded engineering assumptions.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Both endpoints, semantics and validity interval/configuration must be identified.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Cross-boundary assumptions require two-sided confirmation and composition evidence.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Material assumptions need a falsification, monitoring or validation plan; trace semantics need sampling/challenge.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** MIXED
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** STRONG_WITH_TRIGGER_AND_CHEAP_PATH
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Requirements completeness versus discovery** — balancing properties: P006, P008, P012; hybrid: Progressive elaboration; assumptions; scenarios; unknown/TBD inventory; risk-ordered refinement.
- **Model automation versus analyst judgement** — balancing properties: P047, P057; hybrid: Automation proposes/checks; humans own authority, assumptions, semantics and consequential acceptance; sampled audits.

#### ARCHITECTURE_DECISION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Manage assumptions as owned, testable and expiring engineering claims
- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **DRIVERS:** Reuse; supplier boundaries; uncertain environment; models/simulation; long lifecycle; autonomous constituents.
- **DECISION:** Change analyst, maintainer, architect, verifier, integration lead and assurance authority.
- **ALTERNATIVES:** Do not invent an architecture decision unless the property changes a consequential option.
- **RATIONALE:** Represent each material assumption with owner, source, affected claims, test/monitor, expiry/review condition and response if false.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Defined relation types, owners, source identities, current configurations and real decisions that consume the relation.
- **AUTHORITY:** Someone must be authorised to create, challenge, expire and waive the relation or assumption.
- **CONFIGURATION:** Both endpoints, semantics and validity interval/configuration must be identified.
- **EVALUATION:** Material assumptions need a falsification, monitoring or validation plan; trace semantics need sampling/challenge.
- **REOPEN_TRIGGER:** Reopen when the source, assumption, interface, configuration or evidence boundary changes materially.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For local reversible work, state the one or two assumptions that could overturn the decision and test them directly.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Assumption currentness is a first-class relation separate from ordinary traceability, with explicit falsification and change propagation.

#### INTERFACE_ASSUMPTION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Manage assumptions as owned, testable and expiring engineering claims
- **INTERFACE_SCOPE:** Physical, logical, data, timing, state/mode, protocol, organisational, authority and environmental dimensions as applicable.
- **OWNER:** Both endpoints plus joint resolution/escalation authority; a document custodian alone is insufficient.
- **SEMANTICS:** Units, ranges, meaning, timing, failure/degraded behaviour and provenance are explicit at risk-appropriate precision.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Cross-boundary assumptions are owned, current and testable/monitorable.
- **VERSION_IDENTITY:** Endpoint, contract, tool/data and environment versions are bound to evidence.
- **CHANGE_NOTIFICATION:** Change impact, compatibility window and revalidation responsibilities are explicit.
- **VERIFICATION:** Check each side's conformance to the contract.
- **INTEGRATION:** Exercise composition and end-to-end interactions in a credible context.
- **DEGRADATION:** Fallback, error handling and residual incompatibility are represented where consequential.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For local reversible work, state the one or two assumptions that could overturn the decision and test them directly.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Assumption currentness is a first-class relation separate from ordinary traceability, with explicit falsification and change propagation.

#### CONFIGURATION_CHANGE_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Manage assumptions as owned, testable and expiring engineering claims
- **IDENTIFIED_ITEMS:** All representations/products/environments whose identity can change the claim or decision.
- **BASELINE_OR_CURRENT_STATE:** Authoritative current composition and supersession are explicit; a baseline is not presumed frozen forever.
- **CHANGE_AUTHORITY:** Someone must be authorised to create, challenge, expire and waive the relation or assumption.
- **IMPACT_SCOPE:** Requirements, architecture, assumptions, interfaces, suppliers, models, code/hardware, tests, operations and claims as material.
- **STATUS_ACCOUNTING:** Decision, implementation, rollout, exception and actual operational state are distinguished.
- **EVIDENCE_CURRENTNESS:** Evidence cites configuration and has invalidation/reuse rules.
- **ROLLBACK_OR_RECOVERY:** Required where change consequence and reversibility justify it.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For local reversible work, state the one or two assumptions that could overturn the decision and test them directly.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Assumption currentness is a first-class relation separate from ordinary traceability, with explicit falsification and change propagation.

#### INTEGRATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Manage assumptions as owned, testable and expiring engineering claims
- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **INTEGRATION_OBJECTIVE:** Reduces the probability, detection latency or consequence of: Ariane-style reuse, interface mismatch or invalid model inference occurs while all explicit requirements appear satisfied. It should also improve the quality or speed of the named decision consumer; otherwise it should be retired or simplified.
- **SEQUENCE:** Order work by risk, dependency and learning only where composition is part of the failure mechanism.
- **ENTRY_IDENTITY:** Both endpoints, semantics and validity interval/configuration must be identified.
- **ENVIRONMENT_OR_SURROGATE:** Material assumptions need a falsification, monitoring or validation plan; trace semantics need sampling/challenge.
- **INTERFACE_TARGETS:** Cross-boundary assumptions require two-sided confirmation and composition evidence.
- **ANOMALY_FLOW:** Feed relevant anomalies back to the governing source, decision or assumption.
- **REGRESSION:** Select after change impact and prior failure mechanisms.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For local reversible work, state the one or two assumptions that could overturn the decision and test them directly.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Assumption currentness is a first-class relation separate from ordinary traceability, with explicit falsification and change propagation.

#### VERIFICATION_VALIDATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Manage assumptions as owned, testable and expiring engineering claims
- **CLAIM:** A specific requirement, architecture/interface proposition, model-use claim, mission/use claim or change-equivalence claim.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does it fulfil the current intended use/need in the relevant context?
- **METHOD:** Test, analysis, inspection, demonstration, simulation, operational evidence or combination selected for discrimination and cost.
- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** Representative enough for the claim; provenance, fidelity and limitations explicit.
- **INDEPENDENCE:** Proportionate to consequence, producer incentives and claim uncertainty.
- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** Coverage is stated but never equated with absence of unknown behaviour.
- **ANOMALIES:** Contradictory and unresolved evidence remains visible.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Bound to configuration, assumptions, intended use and change impact.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For local reversible work, state the one or two assumptions that could overturn the decision and test them directly.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Assumption currentness is a first-class relation separate from ordinary traceability, with explicit falsification and change propagation.

#### MODEL_OR_DIGITAL_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Manage assumptions as owned, testable and expiring engineering claims
- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Named question, decision or evidence use; no value claim from existence or volume.
- **AUTHORITATIVE_SCOPE:** Authority is claim- and use-specific, never automatic truth.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** Permissible decisions, assumptions, uncertainty, fidelity and caveats.
- **PROVENANCE:** Model/metamodel/tool/version, data and transformations are identified.
- **PARITY:** Implementation/physical/test/environment relations and permitted divergence are explicit.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Semantic mapping and transformation losses are tested; open export/API and exit path considered.
- **VALIDATION:** Credibility evidence proportionate to decision consequence.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Refresh latency, event triggers and stale-state signalling are explicit.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For local reversible work, state the one or two assumptions that could overturn the decision and test them directly.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Assumption currentness is a first-class relation separate from ordinary traceability, with explicit falsification and change propagation.

### P011 — Maintain semantic, consumer-driven traceability

- **FAILURE_MODE:** A complete matrix gives false confidence while engineers still cannot answer source, consequence or evidence questions.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A current semantic relation with a named consumer and invalidation rules, independent of spreadsheet or model tooling.
- **TRIGGER:** Many artefacts/teams; high change cost; certification; maintenance; cross-level impact analysis.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For small co-located work, deterministic naming, version control and direct inspection may answer the needed question more cheaply.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Someone must be authorised to create, challenge, expire and waive the relation or assumption.
- **CRITICISMS:** Trace maintenance cost, tool fragmentation and collaboration barriers are substantial; automated links can produce false positives.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Trace matrices and assumption logs are optional. The property is a current, meaningful and challengeable relation.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S057`, `S058`, `S062`, `S067`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S067`, `S057`, `S062`, `S058`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Defined relation types, owners, source identities, current configurations and real decisions that consume the relation.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Relations must ultimately connect to authorised needs or explicitly bounded engineering assumptions.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Both endpoints, semantics and validity interval/configuration must be identified.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Cross-boundary assumptions require two-sided confirmation and composition evidence.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Material assumptions need a falsification, monitoring or validation plan; trace semantics need sampling/challenge.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** MIXED
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Traceability completeness versus maintenance burden** — balancing properties: P044, P052; hybrid: Consumer-defined relation types, automation with semantic checks, expiry and pruning.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Maintain semantic, consumer-driven traceability” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can a complete matrix gives false confidence while engineers still cannot answer source, consequence or evidence questions remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Can a documented and traceable requirement still be unjustified, semantically wrong or stale?
- Does progressive refinement propagate through allocations, decisions and V&V before closure?

### P012 — Justify derived requirements and allocations

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Subsystems meet their assigned numbers while the integrated system misses mission performance.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Allocation is a justified, revisable design decision—not an automatic textual decomposition.
- **TRIGGER:** Multi-level systems; shared resources; supplier contracts; tight budgets; safety/performance constraints.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** A small indivisible implementation can keep a direct design rationale instead of formal allocation trees.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **CRITICISMS:** Top-down flow-down can overconstrain design and conceal emergent interactions; false numerical precision is common.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Templates, shall-statements, databases and RTMs are optional. The property is a justified, current and usable constraint/claim.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S010`, `S064`, `S088`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S010`, `S003`, `S088`, `S064`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** A governed need/source model, identifiable authors/owners, change mechanism and downstream design/V&V consumers.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Requirements must inherit legitimacy from validated need, contract/regulation or explicitly authorised design constraints.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Each requirement set and relation must identify version, baseline, applicability and supersession.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Allocations and cross-boundary requirements require explicit interface ownership and composition checks.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Feasibility and verification method must be considered, while validation remains separate from testability.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Requirements completeness versus discovery** — balancing properties: P007, P010, P059; hybrid: Progressive elaboration; assumptions; scenarios; unknown/TBD inventory; risk-ordered refinement.
- **Design freedom versus requirement constraint** — balancing properties: P002, P019, P021; hybrid: Specify need/performance/constraints before solution; defer allocations; preserve alternatives until evidence discriminates.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Justify derived requirements and allocations” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can subsystems meet their assigned numbers while the integrated system misses mission performance remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Can a documented and traceable requirement still be unjustified, semantically wrong or stale?
- Does progressive refinement propagate through allocations, decisions and V&V before closure?
