- **CHANGE_AUTHORITY:** Issuing, approving, interpreting and waiving authority must be distinguished.
- **IMPACT_SCOPE:** Requirements, architecture, assumptions, interfaces, suppliers, models, code/hardware, tests, operations and claims as material.
- **STATUS_ACCOUNTING:** Decision, implementation, rollout, exception and actual operational state are distinguished.
- **EVIDENCE_CURRENTNESS:** Evidence cites configuration and has invalidation/reuse rules.
- **ROLLBACK_OR_RECOVERY:** Required where change consequence and reversibility justify it.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** A small indivisible implementation can keep a direct design rationale instead of formal allocation trees.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Allocation is a justified, revisable design decision—not an automatic textual decomposition.

#### INTEGRATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Justify derived requirements and allocations
- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **INTEGRATION_OBJECTIVE:** Reduces the probability, detection latency or consequence of: Subsystems meet their assigned numbers while the integrated system misses mission performance. It should also improve the quality or speed of the named decision consumer; otherwise it should be retired or simplified.
- **SEQUENCE:** Order work by risk, dependency and learning only where composition is part of the failure mechanism.
- **ENTRY_IDENTITY:** Each requirement set and relation must identify version, baseline, applicability and supersession.
- **ENVIRONMENT_OR_SURROGATE:** Feasibility and verification method must be considered, while validation remains separate from testability.
- **INTERFACE_TARGETS:** Allocations and cross-boundary requirements require explicit interface ownership and composition checks.
- **ANOMALY_FLOW:** Feed relevant anomalies back to the governing source, decision or assumption.
- **REGRESSION:** Select after change impact and prior failure mechanisms.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** A small indivisible implementation can keep a direct design rationale instead of formal allocation trees.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Allocation is a justified, revisable design decision—not an automatic textual decomposition.

#### VERIFICATION_VALIDATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Justify derived requirements and allocations
- **CLAIM:** A specific requirement, architecture/interface proposition, model-use claim, mission/use claim or change-equivalence claim.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does it fulfil the current intended use/need in the relevant context?
- **METHOD:** Test, analysis, inspection, demonstration, simulation, operational evidence or combination selected for discrimination and cost.
- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** Representative enough for the claim; provenance, fidelity and limitations explicit.
- **INDEPENDENCE:** Proportionate to consequence, producer incentives and claim uncertainty.
- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** Coverage is stated but never equated with absence of unknown behaviour.
- **ANOMALIES:** Contradictory and unresolved evidence remains visible.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Bound to configuration, assumptions, intended use and change impact.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** A small indivisible implementation can keep a direct design rationale instead of formal allocation trees.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Allocation is a justified, revisable design decision—not an automatic textual decomposition.

#### MODEL_OR_DIGITAL_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Justify derived requirements and allocations
- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Named question, decision or evidence use; no value claim from existence or volume.
- **AUTHORITATIVE_SCOPE:** Authority is claim- and use-specific, never automatic truth.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** Permissible decisions, assumptions, uncertainty, fidelity and caveats.
- **PROVENANCE:** Model/metamodel/tool/version, data and transformations are identified.
- **PARITY:** Implementation/physical/test/environment relations and permitted divergence are explicit.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Semantic mapping and transformation losses are tested; open export/API and exit path considered.
- **VALIDATION:** Credibility evidence proportionate to decision consequence.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Refresh latency, event triggers and stale-state signalling are explicit.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** A small indivisible implementation can keep a direct design rationale instead of formal allocation trees.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Allocation is a justified, revisable design decision—not an automatic textual decomposition.

</details>

### P013 — Identify architecture drivers and quality-attribute concerns

- **FAILURE_MODE:** A tidy decomposition optimises function while missing latency, evolvability, safety, reliability, human or interoperability trade-offs.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A current set of decision-bearing drivers with authority, scenarios and measures—not an undifferentiated requirements dump.
- **TRIGGER:** Multiple quality attributes; high coupling; long-life system; critical non-functional behaviour.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For routine low-coupling designs, a short driver list and direct design review are enough.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** Driver selection can mirror the architect's preferences; quality attributes interact and are hard to quantify.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Architecture descriptions, views, boards and models are carriers. The property is consequential structure, rationale, responsibility and evidence.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S011`, `S028`, `S070`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S011`, `S028`, `S070`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Authoritative intent/requirements, known decision horizon, architecture authority, alternatives and access to relevant domain expertise.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Architectural concerns must be grounded in stakeholder/mission outcomes rather than architect preference.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The decision, representation and implemented configuration must be related and versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Responsibilities, interfaces, shared resources and cross-cutting interactions must be analysed.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Consequential claims require scenario, prototype, analysis, model or integration evidence and reopening conditions.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_TO_STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Upfront architecture versus evolutionary learning** — balancing properties: P007, P021, P059; hybrid: Set-based alternatives; minimum architecture runway; explicit decision-reopening triggers; incremental integration.
- **Modularity versus cross-cutting optimisation** — balancing properties: P016, P055; hybrid: Explicit coupling budgets; platform rules; exceptions with rationale; system-level optimisation and integration evidence.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Identify architecture drivers and quality-attribute concerns” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can a tidy decomposition optimises function while missing latency, evolvability, safety, reliability, human or interoperability trade-offs remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Are architecture alternatives, assumptions, quality concerns and rationale recoverable, or only views/diagrams?
- What evidence would reopen the decision after integration or operational learning?

### P014 — Record architecture decisions, alternatives and rationale

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Later teams cannot assess change, debt or reuse and either preserve obsolete structure or repeat the decision blindly.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Decision-centred architecture knowledge linked to current implementation and evidence; diagrams are optional views.
- **TRIGGER:** Long-lived systems; personnel turnover; costly choices; multiple alternatives; reuse/platform decisions.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible choice, a concise decision note with the rejected alternative and trigger is sufficient.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** Rationale capture is costly, becomes stale and may rationalise decisions after the fact.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Architecture descriptions, views, boards and models are carriers. The property is consequential structure, rationale, responsibility and evidence.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S024`, `S068`, `S070`, `S072`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S072`, `S024`, `S068`, `S070`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Authoritative intent/requirements, known decision horizon, architecture authority, alternatives and access to relevant domain expertise.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Architectural concerns must be grounded in stakeholder/mission outcomes rather than architect preference.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The decision, representation and implemented configuration must be related and versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Responsibilities, interfaces, shared resources and cross-cutting interactions must be analysed.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Consequential claims require scenario, prototype, analysis, model or integration evidence and reopening conditions.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_TO_STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** STRONG_WITH_TRIGGER_AND_CHEAP_PATH
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Upfront architecture versus evolutionary learning** — balancing properties: P007, P021, P059; hybrid: Set-based alternatives; minimum architecture runway; explicit decision-reopening triggers; incremental integration.
- **Architecture governance versus team autonomy** — balancing properties: P021, P044; hybrid: Guardrails and interfaces centrally governed; local implementation delegated; exception path evidence-based.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Record architecture decisions, alternatives and rationale” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can later teams cannot assess change, debt or reuse and either preserve obsolete structure or repeat the decision blindly remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Are architecture alternatives, assumptions, quality concerns and rationale recoverable, or only views/diagrams?
- What evidence would reopen the decision after integration or operational learning?

### P015 — Decompose responsibilities while preserving interaction reasoning

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Every component passes while the composition fails.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Dual representation: clear responsibility boundaries plus interaction/composition analysis and integration evidence.
- **TRIGGER:** Many components/teams; organisational distribution; complex interactions; shared budgets or state.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Simple low-coupling work may use direct whole-system reasoning without a hierarchy.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** Over-decomposition creates handoffs, duplicate abstractions and false independence; under-decomposition overwhelms cognition.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Architecture descriptions, views, boards and models are carriers. The property is consequential structure, rationale, responsibility and evidence.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S011`, `S092`, `S093`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S011`, `S092`, `S003`, `S093`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Authoritative intent/requirements, known decision horizon, architecture authority, alternatives and access to relevant domain expertise.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Architectural concerns must be grounded in stakeholder/mission outcomes rather than architect preference.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The decision, representation and implemented configuration must be related and versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Responsibilities, interfaces, shared resources and cross-cutting interactions must be analysed.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Consequential claims require scenario, prototype, analysis, model or integration evidence and reopening conditions.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_TO_STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Decomposition versus emergent interactions** — balancing properties: P035; hybrid: Decompose ownership but preserve interaction models, mission threads, composition tests and system-level measures.
- **Modularity versus cross-cutting optimisation** — balancing properties: P016, P055; hybrid: Explicit coupling budgets; platform rules; exceptions with rationale; system-level optimisation and integration evidence.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Decompose responsibilities while preserving interaction reasoning” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can every component passes while the composition fails remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Are architecture alternatives, assumptions, quality concerns and rationale recoverable, or only views/diagrams?
- What evidence would reopen the decision after integration or operational learning?

- **INTEGRATION_OBJECTIVE:** Expose the highest-value composition uncertainty early enough to change design/action.

- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does it fulfil the current intended use/need in the relevant context?

- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Named question, decision or evidence use; no value claim from existence or volume.

- **PROVENANCE:** Model/metamodel/tool/version, data and transformations are identified.
- **PARITY:** Implementation/physical/test/environment relations and permitted divergence are explicit.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Semantic mapping and transformation losses are tested; open export/API and exit path considered.
- **VALIDATION:** Credibility evidence proportionate to decision consequence.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Refresh latency, event triggers and stale-state signalling are explicit.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Simple low-coupling work may use direct whole-system reasoning without a hierarchy.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Dual representation: clear responsibility boundaries plus interaction/composition analysis and integration evidence.

### P016 — Demonstrate modularity, coupling and evolvability rather than merely claiming them

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Promised change independence, reuse or supplier substitutability fails in practice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Scenario- and evidence-based modularity with measured propagation and explicit cross-cutting constraints.
- **TRIGGER:** Platforms, product families, expected evolution, supplier replacement, parallel development.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For one-off short-life systems with little expected change, avoid elaborate modularity analysis beyond obvious fault/change boundaries.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** Modularity can sacrifice global optimisation, create interface overhead and shift coupling into data/operations.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Architecture descriptions, views, boards and models are carriers. The property is consequential structure, rationale, responsibility and evidence.

### P017 — Evaluate architecture against scenarios and evidence before irreversible commitment

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Major structural risks emerge only after implementation/integration.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-bearing architecture evaluation targeted at decision uncertainty, not a universal board or checklist.
- **TRIGGER:** Novel architecture; high consequence; expensive rework; competing quality attributes; uncertain technology.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Routine architecture with close precedent can use targeted review of deltas and deterministic checks.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** Evaluation methods are workshop- and expert-dependent; they can miss unknown scenarios and become gate theatre.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Architecture descriptions, views, boards and models are carriers. The property is consequential structure, rationale, responsibility and evidence.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S012`, `S070`, `S073`, `S047`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S012`, `S070`, `S073`, `S047`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Authoritative intent/requirements, known decision horizon, architecture authority, alternatives and access to relevant domain expertise.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Architectural concerns must be grounded in stakeholder/mission outcomes rather than architect preference.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The decision, representation and implemented configuration must be related and versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Responsibilities, interfaces, shared resources and cross-cutting interactions must be analysed.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Consequential claims require scenario, prototype, analysis, model or integration evidence and reopening conditions.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_TO_STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Upfront architecture versus evolutionary learning** — balancing properties: P007, P021, P059; hybrid: Set-based alternatives; minimum architecture runway; explicit decision-reopening triggers; incremental integration.
- **Architecture governance versus team autonomy** — balancing properties: P021, P044; hybrid: Guardrails and interfaces centrally governed; local implementation delegated; exception path evidence-based.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Evaluate architecture against scenarios and evidence before irreversible commitment” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can major structural risks emerge only after implementation/integration remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?

- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Are architecture alternatives, assumptions, quality concerns and rationale recoverable, or only views/diagrams?

- **NEED_OR_SOURCE:** Must resolve to current stakeholder/mission evidence or an explicitly authorised constraint; for rejected candidates, this is the missing stronger relation.
- **REQUIREMENT_FORM:** Structured text, model, contract, scenario, constraint, testable property or other fit-for-purpose representation; no mandatory template.
- **PROVENANCE:** Source identity, version, rationale and derivation are required where the property can govern downstream work.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Material assumptions are explicit, owned and linked to falsification/expiry conditions.
- **AUTHORITY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **TRACEABILITY:** Only semantic relations needed by source, impact, implementation or evidence consumers; link presence alone is insufficient.
- **VALIDATION_PRECONDITION:** The governing need and intended use remain current enough to justify the requirement.
- **VERIFICATION_PRECONDITION:** The claim and applicable configuration are precise enough for a discriminating evidence method.
- **CHANGE_PROFILE:** Versioned; impacts and evidence are invalidated/reconfirmed after relevant source, assumption, architecture, interface or configuration change.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Routine architecture with close precedent can use targeted review of deltas and deterministic checks.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-bearing architecture evaluation targeted at decision uncertainty, not a universal board or checklist.

#### ARCHITECTURE_DECISION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Evaluate architecture against scenarios and evidence before irreversible commitment
- **DRIVERS:** Current mission/stakeholder outcomes, hard constraints, quality attributes, interface/evolution risks and evidence gaps.
- **DECISION:** Use consequence-proportionate scenarios, prototypes, models, independent challenge and trade-off analysis; record risks and what evidence would retire them.
- **ALTERNATIVES:** Materially different feasible options, including status quo/reference where informative; no token alternatives.
- **RATIONALE:** Decision context, criteria, evidence, uncertainty, rejected alternatives and consequences.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Owned, current, linked to the decision and given reopening/falsification triggers.
- **AUTHORITY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **CONFIGURATION:** Architecture decision and views/models identify the applicable product/model/configuration.
- **EVALUATION:** Scenario, analysis, prototype, model, review or integration evidence proportional to consequence.
- **REOPEN_TRIGGER:** Changed intent, invalidated assumption, margin erosion, interface/integration evidence, new alternative or configuration drift.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Routine architecture with close precedent can use targeted review of deltas and deterministic checks.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-bearing architecture evaluation targeted at decision uncertainty, not a universal board or checklist.

#### INTERFACE_ASSUMPTION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Evaluate architecture against scenarios and evidence before irreversible commitment
- **INTERFACE_SCOPE:** Physical, logical, data, timing, state/mode, protocol, organisational, authority and environmental dimensions as applicable.
- **OWNER:** Both endpoints plus joint resolution/escalation authority; a document custodian alone is insufficient.
- **SEMANTICS:** Units, ranges, meaning, timing, failure/degraded behaviour and provenance are explicit at risk-appropriate precision.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Cross-boundary assumptions are owned, current and testable/monitorable.
- **VERSION_IDENTITY:** Endpoint, contract, tool/data and environment versions are bound to evidence.
- **CHANGE_NOTIFICATION:** Change impact, compatibility window and revalidation responsibilities are explicit.
- **VERIFICATION:** Check each side's conformance to the contract.
- **INTEGRATION:** Exercise composition and end-to-end interactions in a credible context.
- **DEGRADATION:** Fallback, error handling and residual incompatibility are represented where consequential.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Routine architecture with close precedent can use targeted review of deltas and deterministic checks.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-bearing architecture evaluation targeted at decision uncertainty, not a universal board or checklist.

#### CONFIGURATION_CHANGE_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Evaluate architecture against scenarios and evidence before irreversible commitment
- **IDENTIFIED_ITEMS:** All representations/products/environments whose identity can change the claim or decision.
- **BASELINE_OR_CURRENT_STATE:** Authoritative current composition and supersession are explicit; a baseline is not presumed frozen forever.
- **CHANGE_AUTHORITY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **IMPACT_SCOPE:** Requirements, architecture, assumptions, interfaces, suppliers, models, code/hardware, tests, operations and claims as material.
- **STATUS_ACCOUNTING:** Decision, implementation, rollout, exception and actual operational state are distinguished.
- **EVIDENCE_CURRENTNESS:** Evidence cites configuration and has invalidation/reuse rules.
- **ROLLBACK_OR_RECOVERY:** Required where change consequence and reversibility justify it.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Routine architecture with close precedent can use targeted review of deltas and deterministic checks.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-bearing architecture evaluation targeted at decision uncertainty, not a universal board or checklist.

#### INTEGRATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Evaluate architecture against scenarios and evidence before irreversible commitment
- **INTEGRATION_OBJECTIVE:** Expose the highest-value composition uncertainty early enough to change design/action.
- **SEQUENCE:** Risk-, dependency- and learning-ordered rather than merely schedule-ordered.
- **ENTRY_IDENTITY:** Exact component/model/software/data/environment versions and readiness evidence.
- **ENVIRONMENT_OR_SURROGATE:** Fidelity, intended use, omissions and correlation are explicit.
- **INTERFACE_TARGETS:** Semantic, timing, state, physical, organisational and failure interactions.
- **ANOMALY_FLOW:** Observation, configuration, hypothesis, disposition, waiver and regression remain linked.
- **REGRESSION:** Selected from change impact and prior failure mechanisms.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Routine architecture with close precedent can use targeted review of deltas and deterministic checks.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-bearing architecture evaluation targeted at decision uncertainty, not a universal board or checklist.

#### VERIFICATION_VALIDATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Evaluate architecture against scenarios and evidence before irreversible commitment
- **CLAIM:** A specific requirement, architecture/interface proposition, model-use claim, mission/use claim or change-equivalence claim.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does it fulfil the current intended use/need in the relevant context?
- **METHOD:** Test, analysis, inspection, demonstration, simulation, operational evidence or combination selected for discrimination and cost.
- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** Representative enough for the claim; provenance, fidelity and limitations explicit.
- **INDEPENDENCE:** Proportionate to consequence, producer incentives and claim uncertainty.
- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** Coverage is stated but never equated with absence of unknown behaviour.
- **ANOMALIES:** Contradictory and unresolved evidence remains visible.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Bound to configuration, assumptions, intended use and change impact.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Routine architecture with close precedent can use targeted review of deltas and deterministic checks.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-bearing architecture evaluation targeted at decision uncertainty, not a universal board or checklist.

#### MODEL_OR_DIGITAL_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Evaluate architecture against scenarios and evidence before irreversible commitment
- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Named question, decision or evidence use; no value claim from existence or volume.
- **AUTHORITATIVE_SCOPE:** Authority is claim- and use-specific, never automatic truth.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** Permissible decisions, assumptions, uncertainty, fidelity and caveats.
- **PROVENANCE:** Model/metamodel/tool/version, data and transformations are identified.
- **PARITY:** Implementation/physical/test/environment relations and permitted divergence are explicit.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Semantic mapping and transformation losses are tested; open export/API and exit path considered.
- **VALIDATION:** Credibility evidence proportionate to decision consequence.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Refresh latency, event triggers and stale-state signalling are explicit.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Routine architecture with close precedent can use targeted review of deltas and deterministic checks.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Evidence-bearing architecture evaluation targeted at decision uncertainty, not a universal board or checklist.

</details>

### P018 — Detect and manage architecture, requirements and model debt/erosion

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Change becomes progressively slower and riskier; teams optimise around undocumented constraints.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A bounded obligation/change-cost model tied to actual consequences and representation parity, not a metaphorical backlog.
- **TRIGGER:** Long-lived/evolving systems; repeated exceptions; platform reuse; model/code divergence.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Do not create a debt taxonomy for trivial clean-up: fix immediately or record one actionable item.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Decision ownership, approval/waiver authority and affected-team participation must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** SE technical-debt evidence is sparse and domain-limited; the label can rebrand ordinary defects or management disagreement.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Architecture descriptions, views, boards and models are carriers. The property is consequential structure, rationale, responsibility and evidence.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S069`, `S071`, `S072`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S069`, `S071`, `S072`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Authoritative intent/requirements, known decision horizon, architecture authority, alternatives and access to relevant domain expertise.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Architectural concerns must be grounded in stakeholder/mission outcomes rather than architect preference.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** The decision, representation and implemented configuration must be related and versioned.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Responsibilities, interfaces, shared resources and cross-cutting interactions must be analysed.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Consequential claims require scenario, prototype, analysis, model or integration evidence and reopening conditions.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE_TO_STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- No specific ledger tension mapped; generic cost/benefit and tailoring tension still applies.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Detect and manage architecture, requirements and model debt/erosion” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can change becomes progressively slower and riskier; teams optimise around undocumented constraints remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Are architecture alternatives, assumptions, quality concerns and rationale recoverable, or only views/diagrams?
- What evidence would reopen the decision after integration or operational learning?

### P019 — Generate real alternatives before consequential commitment

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Trade studies legitimate predetermined choices and foreclose superior architectures.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Alternative generation is a falsifiable search for materially different feasible means, scaled to decision consequence.
- **TRIGGER:** Architecture down-select; technology selection; high irreversibility; uncertain solution space.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** When only one option is legally/physically possible, verify that constraint rather than fabricate alternatives.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** The analyst recommends; the authorised decision maker decides and owns value judgements.
- **CRITICISMS:** Alternative generation can consume time indefinitely, and political/contract constraints may make the option set fictitious.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Trade matrices and formal studies are optional. The property is an honest, uncertainty-aware comparison that changes a consequential choice.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S024`, `S039`, `S073`, `S074`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S024`, `S039`, `S074`, `S073`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** A legitimate decision frame, decision authority, feasible alternatives, criteria tied to stakeholder value, uncertainty information and decision deadline.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Criteria and constraints must inherit authority from current mission/stakeholder commitments.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Inputs, alternatives, evidence and decision version must be frozen enough to reproduce the argument.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Cross-system consequences and coupled alternatives must be represented where material.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Claims used to discriminate alternatives need fit-for-purpose data, prototypes, analysis or tests.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** STRONG_WITH_TRIGGER_AND_CHEAP_PATH
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** HIGH
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Design freedom versus requirement constraint** — balancing properties: P006, P012; hybrid: Specify need/performance/constraints before solution; defer allocations; preserve alternatives until evidence discriminates.
- **Early commitment versus option preservation** — balancing properties: P026, P031; hybrid: Set-based maturation, staged commitments, option value, decision deadlines and reopen triggers.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Generate real alternatives before consequential commitment” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can trade studies legitimate predetermined choices and foreclose superior architectures remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Are architecture alternatives, assumptions, quality concerns and rationale recoverable, or only views/diagrams?
- What evidence would reopen the decision after integration or operational learning?

- **EVIDENCE_CURRENTNESS:** Claims used to discriminate alternatives need fit-for-purpose data, prototypes, analysis or tests.
- **ROLLBACK_OR_RECOVERY:** Use only where the property's trigger makes recovery or reversion consequential.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** When only one option is legally/physically possible, verify that constraint rather than fabricate alternatives.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Alternative generation is a falsifiable search for materially different feasible means, scaled to decision consequence.

#### INTEGRATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Generate real alternatives before consequential commitment
- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **INTEGRATION_OBJECTIVE:** Reduces the probability, detection latency or consequence of: Trade studies legitimate predetermined choices and foreclose superior architectures. It should also improve the quality or speed of the named decision consumer; otherwise it should be retired or simplified.
- **SEQUENCE:** Order work by risk, dependency and learning only where composition is part of the failure mechanism.
- **ENTRY_IDENTITY:** Inputs, alternatives, evidence and decision version must be frozen enough to reproduce the argument.
- **ENVIRONMENT_OR_SURROGATE:** Claims used to discriminate alternatives need fit-for-purpose data, prototypes, analysis or tests.
- **INTERFACE_TARGETS:** Cross-system consequences and coupled alternatives must be represented where material.
- **ANOMALY_FLOW:** Feed relevant anomalies back to the governing source, decision or assumption.
- **REGRESSION:** Select after change impact and prior failure mechanisms.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** When only one option is legally/physically possible, verify that constraint rather than fabricate alternatives.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Alternative generation is a falsifiable search for materially different feasible means, scaled to decision consequence.

- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **CLAIM:** The preferred option is presented as inevitable, with token alternatives constructed after criteria are known.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the applicable specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does the result remain fit for the current intended use when this property affects that judgement?

- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** Inputs, alternatives, evidence and decision version must be frozen enough to reproduce the argument.
- **INDEPENDENCE:** Proportionate to consequence, producer incentive and claim opacity.
- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** No method ensures the best unimagined alternative is found.
- **ANOMALIES:** Predetermined alternatives; hidden criteria; weight gaming; false precision; stale inputs; analysis paralysis; unrecorded reversibility.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Reconfirm after relevant source, configuration, interface, environment or use change.

### P020 — Test trade decisions for uncertainty, sensitivity and false precision

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Decision makers mistake arithmetic output for objective truth.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Sensitivity-first decision analysis with explicit judgement, uncertainty and robustness; matrices are optional calculators.
- **TRIGGER:** Multi-attribute choices; uncertain data; close alternatives; political stakeholder weights.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** A cheap deterministic discriminator can end the study immediately when one option violates a hard constraint or clearly dominates.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** The analyst recommends; the authorised decision maker decides and owns value judgements.
- **CRITICISMS:** Weighted sums invite gaming, double-counting, arbitrary normalisation and post hoc criteria.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Trade matrices and formal studies are optional. The property is an honest, uncertainty-aware comparison that changes a consequential choice.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S039`, `S073`, `S075`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S075`, `S039`, `S073`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** A legitimate decision frame, decision authority, feasible alternatives, criteria tied to stakeholder value, uncertainty information and decision deadline.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Criteria and constraints must inherit authority from current mission/stakeholder commitments.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Inputs, alternatives, evidence and decision version must be frozen enough to reproduce the argument.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Cross-system consequences and coupled alternatives must be represented where material.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Claims used to discriminate alternatives need fit-for-purpose data, prototypes, analysis or tests.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** HIGH
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- No specific ledger tension mapped; generic cost/benefit and tailoring tension still applies.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Test trade decisions for uncertainty, sensitivity and false precision” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can decision makers mistake arithmetic output for objective truth remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Are architecture alternatives, assumptions, quality concerns and rationale recoverable, or only views/diagrams?
- What evidence would reopen the decision after integration or operational learning?

### P021 — Preserve options, reversibility and decision-reopening triggers

- **FAILURE_MODE:** A locally rational early choice creates expensive path dependence.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Economically bounded option preservation tied to learning value and decision horizon.
- **TRIGGER:** High uncertainty; irreversible manufacturing/infrastructure; immature technology; close alternatives.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Low-cost reversible decisions should be made quickly, observed and changed rather than subjected to elaborate option portfolios.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** The analyst recommends; the authorised decision maker decides and owns value judgements.
- **CRITICISMS:** Set-based methods can multiply modelling/prototyping cost and diffuse accountability.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** Trade matrices and formal studies are optional. The property is an honest, uncertainty-aware comparison that changes a consequential choice.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S073`, `S074`, `S081`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S081`, `S074`, `S073`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** A legitimate decision frame, decision authority, feasible alternatives, criteria tied to stakeholder value, uncertainty information and decision deadline.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Criteria and constraints must inherit authority from current mission/stakeholder commitments.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Inputs, alternatives, evidence and decision version must be frozen enough to reproduce the argument.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Cross-system consequences and coupled alternatives must be represented where material.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Claims used to discriminate alternatives need fit-for-purpose data, prototypes, analysis or tests.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** HIGH
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Upfront architecture versus evolutionary learning** — balancing properties: P013, P014, P017; hybrid: Set-based alternatives; minimum architecture runway; explicit decision-reopening triggers; incremental integration.
- **Interface standardisation versus local autonomy** — balancing properties: P023, P046; hybrid: Stable minimum contracts, version negotiation, adapters, compatibility windows and federated governance.
- **Design freedom versus requirement constraint** — balancing properties: P006, P012; hybrid: Specify need/performance/constraints before solution; defer allocations; preserve alternatives until evidence discriminates.
- **Early commitment versus option preservation** — balancing properties: P026, P031; hybrid: Set-based maturation, staged commitments, option value, decision deadlines and reopen triggers.
- **Architecture governance versus team autonomy** — balancing properties: P014, P017, P058; hybrid: Guardrails and interfaces centrally governed; local implementation delegated; exception path evidence-based.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Preserve options, reversibility and decision-reopening triggers” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can a locally rational early choice creates expensive path dependence remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Are architecture alternatives, assumptions, quality concerns and rationale recoverable, or only views/diagrams?
- What evidence would reopen the decision after integration or operational learning?

- **CLAIM:** Premature commitment locks the architecture around immature technology; conversely, indefinite option preservation delays integration.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the applicable specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does the result remain fit for the current intended use when this property affects that judgement?
- **METHOD:** Claims used to discriminate alternatives need fit-for-purpose data, prototypes, analysis or tests.
- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** Inputs, alternatives, evidence and decision version must be frozen enough to reproduce the argument.
- **INDEPENDENCE:** Proportionate to consequence, producer incentive and claim opacity.
- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** Field evidence does not establish universal option-set size or convergence cadence.
- **ANOMALIES:** Predetermined alternatives; hidden criteria; weight gaming; false precision; stale inputs; analysis paralysis; unrecorded reversibility.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Reconfirm after relevant source, configuration, interface, environment or use change.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Low-cost reversible decisions should be made quickly, observed and changed rather than subjected to elaborate option portfolios.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Economically bounded option preservation tied to learning value and decision horizon.

#### MODEL_OR_DIGITAL_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Preserve options, reversibility and decision-reopening triggers
- **APPLICABILITY:** SUPPORTING_OR_NOT_PRIMARY_PROFILE: this property does not create an independent obligation in this profile; apply only where its stated trigger intersects the profile.
- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Architecture/technology selection authority and stakeholders bearing the consequences.
- **AUTHORITATIVE_SCOPE:** The analyst recommends; the authorised decision maker decides and owns value judgements.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** Field evidence does not establish universal option-set size or convergence cadence.
- **PROVENANCE:** Set-based design and evolutionary/iterative engineering.
- **PARITY:** Inputs, alternatives, evidence and decision version must be frozen enough to reproduce the argument.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Cross-system consequences and coupled alternatives must be represented where material.
- **VALIDATION:** Claims used to discriminate alternatives need fit-for-purpose data, prototypes, analysis or tests.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Signal when source, model, data, configuration or use becomes stale.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Low-cost reversible decisions should be made quickly, observed and changed rather than subjected to elaborate option portfolios.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Economically bounded option preservation tied to learning value and decision horizon.

</details>

### P022 — Assign explicit interface ownership and decision authority

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Interface defects survive because no actor can authoritatively resolve or rebaseline the relation.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Two-sided ownership with an authority path and joint evidence, not a single document custodian.
- **TRIGGER:** Supplier/organisation boundary; independent teams; physical integration; SoS; regulated handoff.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Within one small team, explicit code/data ownership and direct communication may suffice.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Each side's authority and the joint resolution/escalation path must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** Ownership fields can be ceremonial where the owner lacks budget, access or cross-boundary authority.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** ICDs, schemas and APIs are optional carriers. The property is shared, current semantics and two-sided composition evidence.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S043`, `S076`, `S079`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S043`, `S079`, `S003`, `S076`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Identifiable interacting parties/configurations, shared boundary, owners and a means to test or observe composition.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** External/user/ecosystem semantics must reflect intended operations, not only internal schemas.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Both endpoint versions, contract version and environment must be identified.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Physical, logical, data, timing, state, organisational and authority dimensions are considered as applicable.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Interface claims require joint verification/integration evidence under relevant states, limits and failure conditions.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- No specific ledger tension mapped; generic cost/benefit and tailoring tension still applies.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Assign explicit interface ownership and decision authority” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can interface defects survive because no actor can authoritatively resolve or rebaseline the relation remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Does the target track semantic, timing, state, unit and failure assumptions across the interface, or only structural dependencies?
- Can both endpoints pass local checks while the composed behaviour remains wrong?

- **INTEGRATION_OBJECTIVE:** Expose the highest-value composition uncertainty early enough to change design/action.

- **ENTRY_IDENTITY:** Exact component/model/software/data/environment versions and readiness evidence.

- **MATURE_FORM:** Two-sided ownership with an authority path and joint evidence, not a single document custodian.

#### VERIFICATION_VALIDATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Assign explicit interface ownership and decision authority
- **CLAIM:** A specific requirement, architecture/interface proposition, model-use claim, mission/use claim or change-equivalence claim.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does it fulfil the current intended use/need in the relevant context?
- **METHOD:** Test, analysis, inspection, demonstration, simulation, operational evidence or combination selected for discrimination and cost.

- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** Coverage is stated but never equated with absence of unknown behaviour.

- **CURRENTNESS:** Bound to configuration, assumptions, intended use and change impact.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Within one small team, explicit code/data ownership and direct communication may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Two-sided ownership with an authority path and joint evidence, not a single document custodian.

- **PROPERTY:** Assign explicit interface ownership and decision authority
- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Named question, decision or evidence use; no value claim from existence or volume.
- **AUTHORITATIVE_SCOPE:** Authority is claim- and use-specific, never automatic truth.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** Permissible decisions, assumptions, uncertainty, fidelity and caveats.
- **PROVENANCE:** Model/metamodel/tool/version, data and transformations are identified.
- **PARITY:** Implementation/physical/test/environment relations and permitted divergence are explicit.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Semantic mapping and transformation losses are tested; open export/API and exit path considered.
- **VALIDATION:** Credibility evidence proportionate to decision consequence.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Refresh latency, event triggers and stale-state signalling are explicit.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** Within one small team, explicit code/data ownership and direct communication may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Two-sided ownership with an authority path and joint evidence, not a single document custodian.

### P023 — Specify interface semantics, timing, state and failure contracts

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Components interoperate nominally but fail under real sequences, loads or exceptions.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A current behavioural/semantic contract whose precision is proportionate to risk; documents, schemas, types and tests are interchangeable carriers.
- **TRIGGER:** Cross-team integration; data exchange; real-time systems; safety/performance coupling; supplier APIs.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For obvious low-risk local interfaces, executable types/tests and one shared example may be enough.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Each side's authority and the joint resolution/escalation path must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** Formal contracts can omit relevant environment or human meaning; over-specification can block evolution.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** ICDs, schemas and APIs are optional carriers. The property is shared, current semantics and two-sided composition evidence.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S076`, `S077`, `S078`, `S043`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S043`, `S003`, `S076`, `S078`

- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Identifiable interacting parties/configurations, shared boundary, owners and a means to test or observe composition.

- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Both endpoint versions, contract version and environment must be identified.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Physical, logical, data, timing, state, organisational and authority dimensions are considered as applicable.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Interface claims require joint verification/integration evidence under relevant states, limits and failure conditions.

- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED

- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** ICDs, schemas and APIs are optional carriers. The property is shared, current semantics and two-sided composition evidence.

- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** STRONG_WITH_TRIGGER_AND_CHEAP_PATH
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- No specific ledger tension mapped; generic cost/benefit and tailoring tension still applies.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Verify interface assumptions and currentness” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can configuration-compatible components still fail in combination remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Does the target track semantic, timing, state, unit and failure assumptions across the interface, or only structural dependencies?
- Can both endpoints pass local checks while the composed behaviour remains wrong?

### P025 — Obtain cross-boundary interface and integration evidence

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Late system test discovers composition defects that neither component owner can reproduce alone.
- **MATURE_FORM:** A risk-ranked interface evidence set tied to both sides' exact configurations and the intended operating context.
- **TRIGGER:** Critical external interfaces; multiple suppliers; hardware/software/human boundaries; SoS interoperability.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For low-risk local interfaces, executable contract tests and one end-to-end smoke path may settle the question.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Each side's authority and the joint resolution/escalation path must be explicit.
- **CRITICISMS:** Shared tests can be brittle, expensive or falsely reassuring if the environment and states are narrow.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** ICDs, schemas and APIs are optional carriers. The property is shared, current semantics and two-sided composition evidence.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S042`, `S043`, `S076`, `S083`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S043`, `S042`, `S076`, `S083`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Identifiable interacting parties/configurations, shared boundary, owners and a means to test or observe composition.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** External/user/ecosystem semantics must reflect intended operations, not only internal schemas.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** Both endpoint versions, contract version and environment must be identified.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Physical, logical, data, timing, state, organisational and authority dimensions are considered as applicable.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Interface claims require joint verification/integration evidence under relevant states, limits and failure conditions.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH

- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG

### P026 — Establish authoritative configuration identity and baselines

- **FAILURE_MODE:** Evidence is attributed to an indeterminate or non-reproducible configuration.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Identity sufficient to answer exactly what was decided, built, integrated, tested, delivered and operated—without mandating frozen documents.
- **TRIGGER:** Multiple teams/suppliers; releases/builds; physical units; certification; long lifecycle; evidence reuse.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible prototype, an immutable build identifier, dependency lock and dated test record may suffice.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Authority to baseline, change, waive, release, roll back and supersede must be explicit and consequence-tiered.
- **CRITICISMS:** Baselines can freeze learning, proliferate snapshots or create clerical work without improving reproducibility.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** CCBs, forms and baseline reviews are optional. The property is truthful identity, authority, implementation status and evidence continuity.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S003`, `S034`, `S035`, `S036`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S036`, `S034`, `S003`, `S035`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Governed items, stable identifiers, change authority, status accounting and access to actual product/model/test/environment state.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Configuration must be tied to the authorised capability, commitments and operational population.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** This family centrally requires exact item/composition/version/environment/evidence identity.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Interface and supplier configuration dependencies must be included in impact and release decisions.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Evidence must cite configuration and be invalidated or justified after change.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** STRONG_WITH_TRIGGER_AND_CHEAP_PATH
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Stable baselines versus changing intent** — balancing properties: P001, P007, P059; hybrid: Time-bounded/incremental baselines with named authority, impact propagation and revalidation.
- **Single authoritative representation versus plural specialised models** — balancing properties: P048; hybrid: Authority by claim; federated models; mappings and reconciliation; loss disclosure.
- **Early commitment versus option preservation** — balancing properties: P019, P021; hybrid: Set-based maturation, staged commitments, option value, decision deadlines and reopen triggers.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Establish authoritative configuration identity and baselines” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can evidence is attributed to an indeterminate or non-reproducible configuration remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Can the exact model, implementation, test, data, tool and environment configuration supporting a claim be reconstructed?
- Does change impact invalidate and re-establish all affected evidence rather than merely record approval?

- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Owned, current, linked to the decision and given reopening/falsification triggers.
- **AUTHORITY:** Authority to baseline, change, waive, release, roll back and supersede must be explicit and consequence-tiered.
- **CONFIGURATION:** Architecture decision and views/models identify the applicable product/model/configuration.
- **EVALUATION:** Scenario, analysis, prototype, model, review or integration evidence proportional to consequence.
- **REOPEN_TRIGGER:** Changed intent, invalidated assumption, margin erosion, interface/integration evidence, new alternative or configuration drift.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible prototype, an immutable build identifier, dependency lock and dated test record may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Identity sufficient to answer exactly what was decided, built, integrated, tested, delivered and operated—without mandating frozen documents.

#### INTERFACE_ASSUMPTION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Establish authoritative configuration identity and baselines
- **INTERFACE_SCOPE:** Physical, logical, data, timing, state/mode, protocol, organisational, authority and environmental dimensions as applicable.
- **OWNER:** Both endpoints plus joint resolution/escalation authority; a document custodian alone is insufficient.
- **SEMANTICS:** Units, ranges, meaning, timing, failure/degraded behaviour and provenance are explicit at risk-appropriate precision.
- **ASSUMPTIONS:** Cross-boundary assumptions are owned, current and testable/monitorable.
- **VERSION_IDENTITY:** Endpoint, contract, tool/data and environment versions are bound to evidence.
- **CHANGE_NOTIFICATION:** Change impact, compatibility window and revalidation responsibilities are explicit.
- **VERIFICATION:** Check each side's conformance to the contract.
- **INTEGRATION:** Exercise composition and end-to-end interactions in a credible context.
- **DEGRADATION:** Fallback, error handling and residual incompatibility are represented where consequential.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible prototype, an immutable build identifier, dependency lock and dated test record may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Identity sufficient to answer exactly what was decided, built, integrated, tested, delivered and operated—without mandating frozen documents.

#### CONFIGURATION_CHANGE_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Establish authoritative configuration identity and baselines
- **IDENTIFIED_ITEMS:** All representations/products/environments whose identity can change the claim or decision.
- **BASELINE_OR_CURRENT_STATE:** Authoritative current composition and supersession are explicit; a baseline is not presumed frozen forever.
- **CHANGE_AUTHORITY:** Authority to baseline, change, waive, release, roll back and supersede must be explicit and consequence-tiered.
- **IMPACT_SCOPE:** Requirements, architecture, assumptions, interfaces, suppliers, models, code/hardware, tests, operations and claims as material.
- **STATUS_ACCOUNTING:** Decision, implementation, rollout, exception and actual operational state are distinguished.
- **EVIDENCE_CURRENTNESS:** Evidence cites configuration and has invalidation/reuse rules.
- **ROLLBACK_OR_RECOVERY:** Required where change consequence and reversibility justify it.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible prototype, an immutable build identifier, dependency lock and dated test record may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Identity sufficient to answer exactly what was decided, built, integrated, tested, delivered and operated—without mandating frozen documents.

#### INTEGRATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Establish authoritative configuration identity and baselines
- **INTEGRATION_OBJECTIVE:** Expose the highest-value composition uncertainty early enough to change design/action.
- **SEQUENCE:** Risk-, dependency- and learning-ordered rather than merely schedule-ordered.
- **ENTRY_IDENTITY:** Exact component/model/software/data/environment versions and readiness evidence.
- **ENVIRONMENT_OR_SURROGATE:** Fidelity, intended use, omissions and correlation are explicit.
- **INTERFACE_TARGETS:** Semantic, timing, state, physical, organisational and failure interactions.
- **ANOMALY_FLOW:** Observation, configuration, hypothesis, disposition, waiver and regression remain linked.
- **REGRESSION:** Selected from change impact and prior failure mechanisms.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible prototype, an immutable build identifier, dependency lock and dated test record may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Identity sufficient to answer exactly what was decided, built, integrated, tested, delivered and operated—without mandating frozen documents.

#### VERIFICATION_VALIDATION_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Establish authoritative configuration identity and baselines
- **CLAIM:** A specific requirement, architecture/interface proposition, model-use claim, mission/use claim or change-equivalence claim.
- **VERIFICATION_QUESTION:** Does the identified configuration satisfy the specified claim?
- **VALIDATION_QUESTION:** Does it fulfil the current intended use/need in the relevant context?
- **METHOD:** Test, analysis, inspection, demonstration, simulation, operational evidence or combination selected for discrimination and cost.
- **ENVIRONMENT_AND_DATA:** Representative enough for the claim; provenance, fidelity and limitations explicit.
- **INDEPENDENCE:** Proportionate to consequence, producer incentives and claim uncertainty.
- **COVERAGE_AND_LIMITS:** Coverage is stated but never equated with absence of unknown behaviour.
- **ANOMALIES:** Contradictory and unresolved evidence remains visible.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Bound to configuration, assumptions, intended use and change impact.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible prototype, an immutable build identifier, dependency lock and dated test record may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Identity sufficient to answer exactly what was decided, built, integrated, tested, delivered and operated—without mandating frozen documents.

#### MODEL_OR_DIGITAL_PROFILE
- **PROPERTY:** Establish authoritative configuration identity and baselines
- **MODEL_OR_DATA_PURPOSE:** Named question, decision or evidence use; no value claim from existence or volume.
- **AUTHORITATIVE_SCOPE:** Authority is claim- and use-specific, never automatic truth.
- **INTENDED_USE_AND_LIMITS:** Permissible decisions, assumptions, uncertainty, fidelity and caveats.
- **PROVENANCE:** Model/metamodel/tool/version, data and transformations are identified.
- **PARITY:** Implementation/physical/test/environment relations and permitted divergence are explicit.
- **INTEROPERABILITY:** Semantic mapping and transformation losses are tested; open export/API and exit path considered.
- **VALIDATION:** Credibility evidence proportionate to decision consequence.
- **CURRENTNESS:** Refresh latency, event triggers and stale-state signalling are explicit.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** For a small reversible prototype, an immutable build identifier, dependency lock and dated test record may suffice.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Identity sufficient to answer exactly what was decided, built, integrated, tested, delivered and operated—without mandating frozen documents.

### P027 — Govern change authority, status and implementation

- **FAILURE_MODE:** The delivered/operated product diverges from the authorised technical definition.
- **MATURE_FORM:** Consequence-tiered authority and truthful status accounting; a standing board is optional.
- **TRIGGER:** Shared baselines; contractual commitments; multiple suppliers; costly/regulated changes.
- **CHEAP_PATH:** A single owner can approve and implement a low-risk change directly if identity and tests remain explicit.
- **AUTHORITY_BOUNDARY:** Authority to baseline, change, waive, release, roll back and supersede must be explicit and consequence-tiered.
- **CRITICISMS:** Boards can become slow gatekeepers, optimise paperwork and diffuse accountability; emergency paths can bypass analysis.
- **ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY:** CCBs, forms and baseline reviews are optional. The property is truthful identity, authority, implementation status and evidence continuity.
- **PRIMARY_SOURCE_IDS:** `S034`, `S035`, `S036`, `S081`
- **CRITICAL_SOURCE_IDS:** `S081`, `S036`, `S034`, `S035`

#### REQUIRED_PRECONDITIONS
- **DEPENDENCIES_OR_PRECONDITIONS:** Governed items, stable identifiers, change authority, status accounting and access to actual product/model/test/environment state.
- **STAKEHOLDER_OR_MISSION_PRECONDITIONS:** Configuration must be tied to the authorised capability, commitments and operational population.
- **CONFIGURATION_OR_IDENTITY_PRECONDITIONS:** This family centrally requires exact item/composition/version/environment/evidence identity.
- **INTERFACE_OR_INTEGRATION_PRECONDITIONS:** Interface and supplier configuration dependencies must be included in impact and release decisions.
- **EVIDENCE_OR_VV_PRECONDITIONS:** Evidence must cite configuration and be invalidated or justified after change.

#### EVIDENCE_STRENGTH
- **HISTORICAL_PROVENANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **STANDARD_OR_GUIDANCE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FORMAL_OR_MODEL_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **EMPIRICAL_COMPARATIVE_STRENGTH:** LIMITED_TO_MODERATE
- **PROJECT_OR_PROGRAMME_CASE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **FIELD_PRACTICE_STRENGTH:** STRONG
- **REPLICATION_OR_MULTI_STUDY_STRENGTH:** LIMITED
- **TRANSFERABILITY_STRENGTH:** MODERATE
- **ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVITY:** MODERATE
- **CONTRARY_EVIDENCE_STRENGTH:** MODERATE

#### POSSIBLE_CONFLICTING_PROPERTY
- **Stable baselines versus changing intent** — balancing properties: P001, P007, P059; hybrid: Time-bounded/incremental baselines with named authority, impact propagation and revalidation.
- **Configuration control versus rapid adaptation** — balancing properties: P044, P051; hybrid: Tiered authority; automated status/impact checks; emergency path; rollback and post-change reconciliation.

#### QUESTIONS_FOR_REPOSITORY_AUDIT
- Does the target system implement “Govern change authority, status and implementation” when its stated trigger is present, or does it only produce a similarly named artefact?
- Can the delivered/operated product diverges from the authorised technical definition remain apparently green after the governing source, assumption or configuration changes?
- Who has authority to create, change, challenge and retire the governing claim, and is that authority distinct from technical truth?
- What real engineering decision, integration action or evidence judgement consumes this property?
- Is the selected implementation the cheapest discriminator/control that can address the failure mode, or is a lower-cost path available?
- Are configuration identity, interface assumptions, currentness and residual limitations preserved strongly enough to support the claimed result?
- Can the exact model, implementation, test, data, tool and environment configuration supporting a claim be reconstructed?
- Does change impact invalidate and re-establish all affected evidence rather than merely record approval?
